    F3. Techniques of integration

    Technique Statement
    Substitution, indefinite \[\int f(g(x))\, g'(x)\, dx = \int f(u)\, du\]
    with \(u = g(x)\), \(du = g'(x)\, dx\)
    Substitution, definite \[\int_{a}^{b}f(g(x))\, g'(x)\, dx = \int_{g(a)}^{g(b)}f(u)\, du\]
    change the limits and do not convert back
    Integration by parts \[\int u\, dv = uv - \int v\, du\]
    \[\int_{a}^{b}u\, dv = \lbrack uv\rbrack_{a}^{b} - \int_{a}^{b}v\, du\]
    Choosing \(u\) (LIATE) first in the list: Logarithmic, Inverse trigonometric, Algebraic, Trigonometric, Exponential
    Cyclic by parts when the original integral returns (for example \(\int e^{x}\,\sin\, x\, dx = \frac{1}{2}e^{x}(\sin\, x - \cos\, x) + C\)), treat it as an unknown and solve
    Tabular by parts for \(\int P(x)e^{kx}\, dx\) or \(\int P(x)\sin\, kx\, dx\): differentiate \(P\) down to zero, integrate the other factor repeatedly, add the diagonal products with alternating signs

    Recognising which technique to use.

    See this in the integrand Reach for
    an inside function whose derivative also appears \(u\)-substitution
    a product of unrelated types (polynomial with exponential, trigonometric or logarithm; a lone \(\ln\, x\) or inverse trigonometric function) integration by parts
    \[\sqrt{a^{2} - x^{2}}\] \[x = a\,\sin\,\theta\]
    \[\sqrt{a^{2} + x^{2}}\] \[x = a\,\tan\,\theta\]
    \[\sqrt{x^{2} - a^{2}}\] \[x = a\,\sec\,\theta\]
    a ratio of polynomials partial fractions (long division first if \(\deg P \geq \deg Q\))
    \(\sin^{2}\) or \(\cos^{2}\) power-reducing identity
    products such as \(\sin\, mx\,\cos\, nx\) product-to-sum identity

    Four-step strategy: simplify the integrand first (expand, split a fraction, use an identity); look for an obvious substitution; classify the integrand by its form; try again after a partial simplification.

    Trigonometric integrals: powers of sine and cosine.

    \[\int\sin^{m}\, x\,\cos^{n}\, x\, dx\]

    Case Method
    \(m\) odd save one \(\sin\, x\), write the rest with \(\sin^{2}\, x = 1 - \cos^{2}\, x\), let \(u = \cos\, x\)
    \(n\) odd save one \(\cos\, x\), write the rest with \(\cos^{2}\, x = 1 - \sin^{2}\, x\), let \(u = \sin\, x\)
    \(m\) and \(n\) both even power-reducing identities, then expand

    Trigonometric integrals: powers of tangent and secant.

    \[\int\tan^{m}\, x\,\sec^{n}\, x\, dx\]

    Case Method
    \(n\) even save \(\sec^{2}\, x\), write the rest with \(\sec^{2}\, x = 1 + \tan^{2}\, x\), let \(u = \tan\, x\)
    \(m\) odd save \(\sec\, x\,\tan\, x\), write the rest with \(\tan^{2}\, x = \sec^{2}\, x - 1\), let \(u = \sec\, x\)
    \(m\) even and \(n\) odd integration by parts or the reduction formula

    Two more to remember.

    \[\int\sec^{3}\, x\, dx = \frac{1}{2}\left( \sec\, x\,\tan\, x + \ln\,|\sec\, x + \tan\, x| \right) + C\]

    For \(\int\sin\, mx\,\cos\, nx\, dx\) and the two similar products, use the product-to-sum identity and integrate term by term.

    Trigonometric substitution.

    Radical Substitution Differential Simplifies to
    \[\sqrt{a^{2} - x^{2}}\] \[x = a\,\sin\,\theta\] \[dx = a\,\cos\,\theta\, d\theta\] \[a\,\cos\,\theta\]
    \[\sqrt{a^{2} + x^{2}}\] \[x = a\,\tan\,\theta\] \[dx = a\,\sec^{2}\,\theta\, d\theta\] \[a\,\sec\,\theta\]
    \[\sqrt{x^{2} - a^{2}}\] \[x = a\,\sec\,\theta\] \[dx = a\,\sec\,\theta\tan\,\theta\, d\theta\] \[a\,\tan\,\theta\]
    Substitution Allowed range of \(\theta\)
    \(x = a\,\sin\,\theta\) \[- \frac{\pi}{2} \leq \theta \leq \frac{\pi}{2}\]
    \(x = a\,\tan\,\theta\) \[- \frac{\pi}{2} < \theta < \frac{\pi}{2}\]
    \(x = a\,\sec\,\theta\) \[0 \leq \theta < \frac{\pi}{2}\]

    After integrating in \(\theta\), convert back to \(x\) with a right triangle labelled from the substitution.

    Partial fractions.

    Factor of \(Q(x)\) Terms to include
    distinct linear \((ax + b)\) \[\frac{A}{ax + b}\]
    repeated linear \((ax + b)^{k}\) \[\frac{A_{1}}{ax + b} + \frac{A_{2}}{(ax + b)^{2}} + \cdots + \frac{A_{k}}{(ax + b)^{k}}\]
    irreducible quadratic \(ax^{2} + bx + c\) \[\frac{Ax + B}{ax^{2} + bx + c}\]
    repeated irreducible quadratic \(\left( ax^{2} + bx + c \right)^{k}\) \[\sum_{i = 1}^{k}\frac{A_{i}x + B_{i}}{\left( ax^{2} + bx + c \right)^{i}}\]

    Solve for the constants by clearing denominators, then plugging in roots of \(Q\) or equating coefficients. Then integrate: \(\int\frac{A}{x - r}\, dx = A\,\ln\,|x - r| + C\), and \(\int\frac{A}{(x - r)^{k}}\, dx = - \frac{A}{(k - 1)(x - r)^{k - 1}} + C\) for \(k > 1\). For an irreducible quadratic, complete the square, then split the numerator into a logarithm piece and an arctangent piece.

    F4. Numerical integration

    Use \(n\) subintervals of width \(\Delta x = \frac{b - a}{n}\) with \(x_{0} = a\), \(x_{i} = a + i\,\Delta x\), \(x_{n} = b\).

    Left and right endpoint rules.

    \[L_{n} = \Delta x\left\lbrack f(x_{0}) + f(x_{1}) + \cdots + f(x_{n - 1}) \right\rbrack,\quad\quad R_{n} = \Delta x\left\lbrack f(x_{1}) + f(x_{2}) + \cdots + f(x_{n}) \right\rbrack\]

    Midpoint rule, with \({\bar{x}}_{i}\) the midpoint of \(\lbrack x_{i - 1},x_{i}\rbrack\).

    \[M_{n} = \Delta x\left\lbrack f({\bar{x}}_{1}) + f({\bar{x}}_{2}) + \cdots + f({\bar{x}}_{n}) \right\rbrack\]

    Trapezoid rule.

    \[T_{n} = \frac{\Delta x}{2}\left\lbrack f(x_{0}) + 2f(x_{1}) + 2f(x_{2}) + \cdots + 2f(x_{n - 1}) + f(x_{n}) \right\rbrack\]

    Simpson’s rule, with \(n\) even.

    \[S_{n} = \frac{\Delta x}{3}\left\lbrack f(x_{0}) + 4f(x_{1}) + 2f(x_{2}) + 4f(x_{3}) + \cdots + 4f(x_{n - 1}) + f(x_{n}) \right\rbrack\]

    The coefficients of Simpson’s rule run \(1,4,2,4,2,\ldots,2,4,1\).

    Error bounds.

    Rule Error bound
    Midpoint \[|E_{M}| \leq \frac{K(b - a)^{3}}{24n^{2}}\]
    Trapezoid \[|E_{T}| \leq \frac{K(b - a)^{3}}{12n^{2}}\]
    Simpson \[|E_{S}| \leq \frac{K(b - a)^{5}}{180n^{4}}\]

    For the midpoint and trapezoid bounds, \(|f''(x)| \leq K\) on \(\lbrack a,b\rbrack\). For Simpson’s rule, \(|f^{(4)}(x)| \leq K\).

    F5. Improper integrals

    Type Definition
    Infinite interval \[\int_{a}^{\infty}f\, dx = \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty}\int_{a}^{t}f\, dx,\quad\int_{- \infty}^{b}f\, dx = \lim_{t \rightarrow - \infty}\int_{t}^{b}f\, dx\]
    converges if the limit is finite
    Both ends infinite split at any \(c\); both \(\int_{- \infty}^{c}f\, dx\) and \(\int_{c}^{\infty}f\, dx\) must converge
    Discontinuity at \(b\) \[\int_{a}^{b}f\, dx = \lim_{t \rightarrow b^{-}}\int_{a}^{t}f\, dx\]
    at \(a\) use \(t \rightarrow a^{+}\); an interior discontinuity is split
    Benchmark, infinite interval \[\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{dx}{x^{p}}\]
    converges if and only if \(p > 1\)
    Benchmark, discontinuity \[\int_{0}^{1}\frac{dx}{x^{p}}\]
    converges if and only if \(p < 1\)
    Comparison theorem \(f(x) \geq g(x) \geq 0\) for \(x \geq a\): if \(\int_{a}^{\infty}f\) converges then so does \(\int_{a}^{\infty}g\); if \(\int_{a}^{\infty}g\) diverges then so does \(\int_{a}^{\infty}f\)
    ↑ Top of this part↑ Appendix top☰ Contents

    G. Applications of Integration

    G1. Area, average value, net change

    Quantity Formula
    Area between curves, vertical slices \[A = \int_{a}^{b}\left\lbrack f(x) - g(x) \right\rbrack dx\]
    where \(f \geq g\) (top minus bottom)
    Area between curves, horizontal slices \[A = \int_{c}^{d}\left\lbrack f(y) - g(y) \right\rbrack dy\]
    where \(x = f(y)\) is right of \(x = g(y)\)
    Curves that cross \[A = \int_{a}^{b}|f(x) - g(x)|\, dx\]
    split at every crossing
    Average value \[f_{\text{avg}} = \frac{1}{b - a}\int_{a}^{b}f(x)\, dx\]
    Mean Value Theorem for integrals \(f\) continuous on \(\lbrack a,b\rbrack\): some \(c\) in \(\lbrack a,b\rbrack\) has \(f(c) = \frac{1}{b - a}\int_{a}^{b}f\, dx\)
    Net change \[\int_{a}^{b}\text{rate}\, dt =\]
    total change in the quantity
    Displacement \[\int_{a}^{b}v(t)\, dt\]
    Distance travelled \[\int_{a}^{b}|v(t)|\, dt\]
    find where \(v = 0\) and split
    Position from velocity \[s(t) = s(0) + \int_{0}^{t}v(u)\, du\]
    likewise \(v\) from \(a\)
    Constant acceleration \[v(t) = v_{0} + at\]
    \[s(t) = s_{0} + v_{0}t + \frac{1}{2}at^{2}\]
    Free fall \[a = - 9.8\ \text{m/s}^{2}\]
    (or \(- 32\ \text{ft/s}^{2}\)), upward positive

    G2. Work

    Situation Formula
    Constant force \[W = Fd\]
    Variable force along a line \[W = \int_{a}^{b}f(x)\, dx\]
    Spring (Hooke’s law) \(f(x) = kx\) \[W = \int_{a}^{b}kx\, dx = \frac{k}{2}\left( b^{2} - a^{2} \right)\]
    from natural length \(W = \frac{1}{2}kd^{2}\)
    Chain or cable \[W = \int\lambda g \cdot (\text{distance lifted})\, dx\]
    where \(\lambda = \frac{\text{mass}}{\text{length}}\) is the density
    Pumping liquid \[W = \int_{c}^{d}\rho g\, A(y)\, d(y)\, dy\]
    with \(A(y)\) the slab area and \(d(y)\) the distance to the outlet
    Water \[\rho = 1000\ \text{kg/m}^{3}\]
    so \(\rho g = 9800\ \text{N/m}^{3}\) (weight density \(62.5\ \text{lb/ft}^{3}\))
    Units newton metre \(=\) joule; foot pound; a mass in kilograms must be multiplied by \(g = 9.8\)

    G3. Volume, arc length, surface area

    Quantity Formula
    Disk method, about the \(x\)-axis \[V = \int_{a}^{b}\pi\left\lbrack f(x) \right\rbrack^{2}dx\]
    Washer method \[V = \int_{a}^{b}\pi\left( R^{2} - r^{2} \right)dx\]
    outer radius \(R\), inner radius \(r\)
    Cylindrical shells, about the \(y\)-axis \[V = \int_{a}^{b}2\pi x\, f(x)\, dx\]
    (radius times height)
    Arc length, \(y = f(x)\) \[L = \int_{a}^{b}\sqrt{1 + \left( \frac{dy}{dx} \right)^{2}}\, dx\]
    Arc length, \(x = g(y)\) \[L = \int_{c}^{d}\sqrt{1 + \left( \frac{dx}{dy} \right)^{2}}\, dy\]
    Arc length, parametric \[L = \int_{\alpha}^{\beta}\sqrt{\left( \frac{dx}{dt} \right)^{2} + \left( \frac{dy}{dt} \right)^{2}}\, dt\]
    Lateral area of a cone \[A = \pi r\ell\]
    Frustum band \[A = 2\pi r\ell\]
    with \(r = \frac{r_{1} + r_{2}}{2}\) the average radius and \(\ell\) the slant height

    Surface of revolution, for \(f\) positive with a continuous derivative.

    Situation Surface area
    Curve \(y = f(x)\), \(a \leq x \leq b\), about the \(x\)-axis \[S = \int_{a}^{b}2\pi f(x)\sqrt{1 + \left\lbrack f'(x) \right\rbrack^{2}}\, dx\]
    the same, written with \(y\) \[S = \int_{a}^{b}2\pi y\sqrt{1 + \left( \frac{dy}{dx} \right)^{2}}\, dx\]
    Curve \(x = g(y)\), \(c \leq y \leq d\), about the \(x\)-axis \[S = \int_{c}^{d}2\pi y\sqrt{1 + \left( \frac{dx}{dy} \right)^{2}}\, dy\]
    About the \(y\)-axis \[S = \int 2\pi x\, ds\]
    About the \(x\)-axis, in symbols \[S = \int 2\pi y\, ds\]
    Arc-length differential \(ds = \sqrt{1 + \left( \frac{dy}{dx} \right)^{2}}\, dx\) or \(ds = \sqrt{1 + \left( \frac{dx}{dy} \right)^{2}}\, dy\)
    \(f\) not necessarily positive \[S = \int_{a}^{b}2\pi|f(x)|\sqrt{1 + \left\lbrack f'(x) \right\rbrack^{2}}\, dx\]
    About the line \(y = c\), for \(f \leq c\) \[S = \int_{a}^{b}2\pi\left\lbrack c - f(x) \right\rbrack\sqrt{1 + \left\lbrack f'(x) \right\rbrack^{2}}\, dx\]
    Zone of a sphere, diameter \(d\), height \(h\) \[S = \pi dh\]

    Think of \(2\pi y\) or \(2\pi x\) as the circumference traced out by the point \((x,y)\) as the curve rotates.

    G4. Setting up any application

    Slice the object into thin pieces, approximate the contribution \(dQ\) of one piece, add the pieces into a Riemann sum that becomes \(\int dQ\), and read the limits from the geometry in the variable the slice was indexed by.

    Application One slice contributes Integral
    Area between curves \[(\text{top} - \text{bottom})\, dx\] \[\int_{a}^{b}\lbrack f - g\rbrack dx\]
    Average value \(f(x)\, dx\), then divide by the width \[\frac{1}{b - a}\int_{a}^{b}f\, dx\]
    Net change from a rate \[r(t)\, dt\] \[\int_{a}^{b}r\, dt\]
    Distance travelled \[|v(t)|\, dt\] \[\int_{a}^{b}|v|\, dt\]
    Work, variable force \[f(x)\, dx\] \[\int_{a}^{b}f\, dx\]
    Work, lifting a slice \[\text{weight} \times \text{distance}\] \[\int\lambda g\, d(x)\, dx\]
    ↑ Top of this part↑ Appendix top☰ Contents

    H. Sequences, Series and Taylor Polynomials

    H1. Basic series

    Series Formula or behaviour
    Geometric series \[\sum_{n = 1}^{\infty}ar^{n - 1} = \frac{a}{1 - r}\]
    converges to the first term over \(1 - r\) if \(|r| < 1\); diverges if \(|r| \geq 1\); the same holds for \(\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}ar^{n}\)
    \(p\)-series \[\sum_{n = 1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{p}}\]
    converges if \(p > 1\); diverges if \(p \leq 1\)
    Harmonic series \[\sum_{n = 1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\]
    diverges (the case \(p = 1\))
    Alternating harmonic series \[\sum_{n = 1}^{\infty}\frac{( - 1)^{n - 1}}{n}\]
    converges conditionally, to \(\ln\, 2\)
    \(n\)th partial sum \[s_{n} = a_{1} + a_{2} + \cdots + a_{n}\]
    the series converges to \(s\) if \(\lim_{n \rightarrow \infty}\, s_{n} = s\)
    Telescoping series write each term as a difference so that the partial sum collapses, then take the limit of \(s_{n}\)

    H2. Convergence tests

    Test Statement
    Test for Divergence if \(\lim_{n \rightarrow \infty}\, a_{n} \neq 0\) or does not exist, then \(\sum a_{n}\) diverges; if the limit is \(0\) the test says nothing
    Integral Test \(f\) positive, continuous, decreasing on \(\lbrack 1,\infty)\) with \(a_{n} = f(n)\): \(\sum a_{n}\) and \(\int_{1}^{\infty}f(x)\, dx\) converge or diverge together
    Comparison Test \(0 \leq a_{n} \leq b_{n}\): if \(\sum b_{n}\) converges so does \(\sum a_{n}\); if \(\sum a_{n}\) diverges so does \(\sum b_{n}\)
    Limit Comparison Test \[a_{n},b_{n} > 0,\quad\lim_{n \rightarrow \infty}\frac{a_{n}}{b_{n}} = c\]
    with \(0 < c < \infty\): both series converge or both diverge
    Alternating Series Test \[\sum( - 1)^{n - 1}b_{n}\]
    with \(b_{n} > 0\), \(b_{n + 1} \leq b_{n}\) and \(\lim_{n \rightarrow \infty}\, b_{n} = 0\) converges; the error satisfies \(|R_{n}| \leq b_{n + 1}\)
    Absolute convergence if \(\sum|a_{n}|\) converges, then \(\sum a_{n}\) converges; if only \(\sum a_{n}\) converges, the convergence is conditional
    Ratio Test \[L = \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty}\left| \frac{a_{n + 1}}{a_{n}} \right|\]
    \(L < 1\) absolutely convergent; \(L > 1\) (or \(\infty\)) divergent; \(L = 1\) inconclusive
    Root Test \[L = \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty}\sqrt[n]{|a_{n}|}\]
    \(L < 1\) absolutely convergent; \(L > 1\) divergent; \(L = 1\) inconclusive

    H3. Strategy for testing a series

    Do not try the tests in a fixed order. Classify the series by its form first.

    If the series Then use
    has the form \(\sum\frac{1}{n^{p}}\) the \(p\)-series rule
    has the form \(\sum ar^{n - 1}\) or \(\sum ar^{n}\) the geometric series rule; some algebra may be needed to reach this form
    is similar to a \(p\)-series or a geometric series a comparison test; for a rational or algebraic function of \(n\), compare with the \(p\)-series made of the highest powers of the top and bottom
    has terms that visibly do not tend to \(0\) the Test for Divergence
    has the form \(\sum( - 1)^{n}b_{n}\) or \(\sum( - 1)^{n - 1}b_{n}\) the Alternating Series Test
    involves factorials or a constant raised to the \(n\)th power the Ratio Test (never for a \(p\)-series or a rational or algebraic function of \(n\), where the ratio tends to \(1\))
    has \(a_{n} = f(n)\) with \(\int_{1}^{\infty}f(x)\, dx\) easy to evaluate the Integral Test
    has terms of mixed sign apply a comparison test to \(|a_{n}|\) and test for absolute convergence

    H4. Power series, Taylor and Maclaurin series

    Item Formula
    Power series about \(a\) \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}c_{n}(x - a)^{n}\]
    the radius \(R\) comes from the ratio test; test the endpoints separately
    Taylor series of \(f\) about \(a\) \[f(x) = \sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{(n)}(a)}{n!}(x - a)^{n}\]
    Maclaurin series the Taylor series with \(a = 0\)
    Taylor polynomial \[T_{n}(x) = \sum_{k = 0}^{n}\frac{f^{(k)}(a)}{k!}(x - a)^{k}\]
    \(T_{1}\) is the linearization \(L(x)\)
    Taylor’s inequality \[|R_{n}(x)| \leq \frac{M}{(n + 1)!}|x - a|^{n + 1}\]
    provided \(|f^{(n + 1)}(x)| \leq M\) for \(|x - a| \leq d\)
    Function Maclaurin series Converges for
    \[\frac{1}{1 - x}\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}x^{n} = 1 + x + x^{2} + x^{3} + \cdots\] \[|x| < 1\]
    \[e^{x}\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{n}}{n!} = 1 + x + \frac{x^{2}}{2!} + \frac{x^{3}}{3!} + \cdots\] all \(x\)
    \[\sin\, x\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{( - 1)^{n}x^{2n + 1}}{(2n + 1)!} = x - \frac{x^{3}}{3!} + \frac{x^{5}}{5!} - \cdots\] all \(x\)
    \[\cos\, x\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{( - 1)^{n}x^{2n}}{(2n)!} = 1 - \frac{x^{2}}{2!} + \frac{x^{4}}{4!} - \cdots\] all \(x\)
    \[\arctan\, x\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{( - 1)^{n}x^{2n + 1}}{2n + 1} = x - \frac{x^{3}}{3} + \frac{x^{5}}{5} - \cdots\] \[|x| \leq 1\]
    \[\ln(1 + x)\] \[\sum_{n = 1}^{\infty}\frac{( - 1)^{n - 1}x^{n}}{n} = x - \frac{x^{2}}{2} + \frac{x^{3}}{3} - \cdots\] \[- 1 < x \leq 1\]
    \[(1 + x)^{k}\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\binom{k}{n}x^{n} = 1 + kx + \frac{k(k - 1)}{2!}x^{2} + \cdots\] \[|x| < 1\]
    \[\sinh\, x\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{2n + 1}}{(2n + 1)!} = x + \frac{x^{3}}{3!} + \cdots\] all \(x\)
    \[\cosh\, x\] \[\sum_{n = 0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{2n}}{(2n)!} = 1 + \frac{x^{2}}{2!} + \cdots\] all \(x\)
    ↑ Top of this part↑ Appendix top☰ Contents

    I. Differential Equations and Fourier Series

    I1. First-order differential equations

    Type Equation, method and solution
    Separable \[\frac{dy}{dx} = g(x)f(y)\]
    separate the variables: \(\int\frac{dy}{f(y)} = \int g(x)\, dx\)
    Exponential growth or decay \[\frac{dy}{dt} = ky\]
    \[y = y_{0}e^{kt}\]
    Newton’s law of cooling \[\frac{dT}{dt} = k(T - T_{s})\]
    \[T = T_{s} + (T_{0} - T_{s})e^{kt}\]
    First-order linear \[\frac{dy}{dx} + P(x)y = Q(x)\]
    integrating factor \(I(x) = e^{\int P(x)\, dx}\), then \(\frac{d}{dx}\left\lbrack I(x)\, y \right\rbrack = I(x)Q(x)\)
    \[y = \frac{1}{I(x)}\left\lbrack \int I(x)Q(x)\, dx + C \right\rbrack\]
    Standard form first if the equation is \(a(x)y' + b(x)y = c(x)\), divide by \(a(x)\) before finding \(P\) and \(Q\)
    Series circuit \[L\frac{dI}{dt} + RI = E(t)\]
    for constant \(E\) and \(I(0) = 0\): \(\quad I(t) = \frac{E}{R}\left( 1 - e^{- Rt/L} \right)\)

    For the linear equation, choose the particular integrating factor with the constant \(A = 1\); there is no need for the most general one.

    I2. Fourier series

    For \(f\) piecewise continuous on \(\lbrack - \pi,\pi\rbrack\) and \(2\pi\)-periodic, the Fourier series is

    \[f(x) \sim a_{0} + \sum_{n = 1}^{\infty}\left( a_{n}\,\cos\, nx + b_{n}\,\sin\, nx \right),\]

    with the Fourier coefficients

    Coefficient Formula
    \(a_{0}\) \[\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}f(x)\, dx\]
    \(a_{n}\), \(n \geq 1\) \[\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}f(x)\cos\, nx\, dx\]
    \(b_{n}\), \(n \geq 1\) \[\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}f(x)\sin\, nx\, dx\]
    Orthogonality integral Value
    \(\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}\,\cos\, nx\, dx\) and \(\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}\,\sin\, nx\, dx\), \(n \geq 1\) \[0\]
    \[\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}\,\cos\, mx\,\cos\, nx\, dx\] \(0\) if \(m \neq n\); \(\pi\) if \(m = n \geq 1\)
    \[\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}\,\sin\, mx\,\sin\, nx\, dx\] \(0\) if \(m \neq n\); \(\pi\) if \(m = n \geq 1\)
    \[\int_{- \pi}^{\pi}\,\sin\, mx\,\cos\, nx\, dx\] \(0\) for all \(m,n\)
    Symmetry Consequence
    \(f\) even every \(b_{n} = 0\); a cosine series
    \(f\) odd \[a_{0} = 0\]
    every \(a_{n} = 0\); a sine series
    At a jump the series converges to the average of the two one-sided limits

    Worked example, the square wave \(f(x) = 0\) for \(- \pi \leq x < 0\) and \(f(x) = 1\) for \(0 \leq x < \pi\):

    \[a_{0} = \frac{1}{2},\quad\quad a_{n} = 0,\quad\quad b_{n} = \frac{1 - ( - 1)^{n}}{n\pi},\quad\quad f(x) \sim \frac{1}{2} + \frac{2}{\pi}\sum_{k = 0}^{\infty}\frac{\sin(2k + 1)x}{2k + 1}.\]
